ParlaMint-AT_2013-03-21-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00194_d8e6820	Madam President. Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) The crime victim law, as we know it, was passed in Austria in 1972 and, of course, since then has been innovative. Austria was a pioneer with Sweden at the time, and we actually went to new countries with the crime victim law at that time. The aim was to offer victims of crimes and their survivors in the event of criminal culprits, regardless of whether physical injury or health damage, to provide assistance, to provide for the acquisition of healing costs and to ensure support for occupational and social rehabilitation. The crime must, as has been said, be illegal, it must be intentional and threatened with a imprisonment of more than six months, then victims of crimes and their survivors will receive assistance under the crime victim law. The competent body in Austria for this purpose is the Federal Social Office and its national offices. There are relief services for victims, including in the replacement of earnings, in health care, for example in psychotherapy. There are also income-dependent supplementary benefits, a care and blind allowance as well as lump sum compensations for pain. There is also assistance for the survivors, as well as assistance for supporters of funeral costs. On the one hand, today's novelle provides for a number of improvements for the victims and also for their survivors. And with this innovation today, there are significant administrative simplifications and also procedural accelerations, which in turn will benefit the victims and survivors in particular. What we're going to decide today is a good law. I would like to address a few points that are decided today in the novel – it is a unanimous matter – more precisely. We will decide on a significant increase in lump sum compensation for pain and further differentiation. There will be four categories in the future: Compensation of between €2,000 and €4,000 in case of serious injury and €8,000 to even €12 000 in case of severe consequences. There will be a cost for victims and survivors in crisis interventions carried out by clinical psychologists or health psychologists and psychologists, up to ten sessions per victim, per victim. In addition, I am particularly grateful to the Minister for this, there will be an involvement of victims of trafficking in human beings. I think this is a group that is particularly disadvantaged. With this novel, these people are also included in the crime victim law. There will be simplified billing criteria in health care for invoices up to €100 per applicant, i.e. in the future, expensive administrative procedures will be avoided, and this, in turn, means that victims and survivors will receive rapid assistance, which will also be available to them. There should be a rigidity scheme for retirement pension rights of detained violent agents, i.e. there will be a cost-taking of victims' entitlements up to the tenfold amount of the compensation allowance rate of a single person; I think there is also a good performance that will be taken into account here. A legal anchorage of those who are recognised by the Judicatur is to be made possible; this is also an important point, which the “White Ring” has repeatedly called for. As I have already said, there is an increase in the replacement of funeral costs, up to €3 300 will be able to pay this maximum compensation. A request from a petition in Parliament, which will improve victims of the so-called home cases in particular, will be accepted today by the Law on Crime Victims. In the future, the decision-making period of the relevant time and no longer the time of action with regard to the right to be punished in criminal law shall be the time of decision. This will surely improve for the victims of the home. There will also be a harmonisation of the time limits for applications by an extension of two years, so that the competence jungle will also be somewhat simplified. I am convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that today we are adopting a very good law, a law that will provide rapid help to victims of crime and their survivors in the future, especially adequate help, although I have already asked in committee: What is appropriate if you become a victim of a crime? Our services can be seen. I would like to express my express thanks to the Federal Minister for this innovation. ==References== Agenda item: It thus becomes the Federal Law, with which the Federal Constitutional Law, the Sacrifice for Care Act, the Crime Sacrifice Law, the Vaccination Law and the 11th. Victim Care Act-Novelle is being amended, treated. We therefore offer a basis of competence for social compensation in the Federal Constitutional Act, i.e. this summary of the principles of competence scattered in the various laws in the new act of competence will lead to a very large simplification. This will simplify procedures, which will in particular also bring about a constitutional adjustment. I am convinced that this will also lead to the rapid implementation of the procedures. What there won't be: There will be no postponement of the legislative and enforcement powers between the Federal Government and countries in the right of social compensation. However, the case-law is summarized in the Federal Constitutional Act in a case-law. It's two good novels that we decide today. I am pleased that this is a unanimous matter.

I don't think it's bad if you have to make an agreement. For other things in labour law, a legal claim is also positive. But here, I think, it is important to agree with the service provider. What is the duration of part-time education? – It shall not be less than four months and shall not exceed two years. Of course, the reduction in working time is essential. It must be at least one quarter, at least 25 percent, and may not exceed 50 percent, so you can reduce your normal working time by a maximum of half. The weekly working time may not be less than 10 hours during this measure. The application, which is also important, must be submitted to the AMS at least four weeks before the agreed start. And during the reference of this money, there must be a genuine employment relationship with a fee, which, of course, is above the low level – which is currently about €386 – in order to obtain this training fee. Students must be shown to have completed examinations during their studies. But people will ask themselves: What do I get, what do I have in the money exchange through this part-time education? Finally, I would like to give you an example that shows that it is really a good measure and that people will also get along with this income. For example, if you currently earn €1 700 net and reduce by half, you get €1 491 net under the line. That means that it is a little less, but in all cases so much that one can dispute the cost of living of it. This was the basic intensification in this part-time education, especially for low and less qualified employees, that they can also continue to train and train themselves during their stay in the company. That is why we welcome this measure from the Austrian People's Party. Minister of the Interior Johanna Mikl- Leitner, Minister of Economic Affairs Reinhold Mitterlehner and, of course, Social Minister Rudolf Hundstorfer have discussed this question in advance and finally achieved that part-time education is being implemented today, an important measure for workers in our country.

That's the new thing in this area. We know the educational career. Two months up to a year you can be released, you get training fees, but you don't stay in the profession. The part-time education, which comes back to today's decision, is based on a reduction in working time. So you do not complete the work, but you reduce it by at least one quarter, at least half of the normal working time. In addition, you will receive a corresponding replacement for the loss of income. It is therefore a partial financial compensation which depends on the reduced number of hours and, of course, on the income level. For example, if you reduce your working time by 50 percent as part-time workers, only a loss of income of about 20 percent remains below the line. On average, you get 80 percent, if you work only half, but you continue to do so. I think it's a good mix that's been found. It's a good law. The proof that it is a good law is also that it has supported all the political groups in the Committee. I assume that this will be the case today in the decision-making process. There is therefore only more left to ask: Let's all advertise for this new model, no matter where we meet with people! Let us point out that we are applying for this new option of further training! I think it's a good advertising we can do through it.

The legislative proposal in 2195 of the Supplements adapts social security procedures to the new administrative jurisdiction. This will make it possible to implement an important administrative simplification and an important and correct reform in social security procedures, as I believe. In detail, it is established and regulated that the Federal Administrative Court now has to decide on complaints against decisions by social security bodies and supervisory authorities. The General Administrative Procedure Act will also be fully applicable to social security institutions. The fact that competence disputes between social security institutions are now decided by the Federal Minister responsible, I think it is a very correct decision. In addition, the competence of the national leaders is transferred to the Federal Minister for Labour, Social Affairs and Consumer Protection, thereby simplifying it. I am convinced, ladies and gentlemen, that this bill will make the procedures in social security law more manageable, more manageable and transparent. What's that supposed to mean? – In the future, the last group of non-employed from third countries will also be included in the agreement. The Contracting States shall continue to have the right to refuse to grant an authorisation for entry, residence, establishment or employment. The application of the Regulation shall apply only if the residence is lawful. The Agreement shall not apply to matters relating only to a single Contracting State, in particular to third-country nationals who have exclusive links with a third country and a single Contracting State. Furthermore, the Agreement does not affect rights and obligations arising from agreements concluded with third countries if the Contracting State is a Party and social security benefits are provided for. ParlaMint-AT_2013-03-21-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00194_d8e12036	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Today's amendment to the Data Protection Act is the result of a decision at European level. The European Court of Justice has criticised the lack of independence from the Committee on Data Protection in Austria. It is out of question if there is a ruling from the ECJ that this is to be implemented in Austria. Nevertheless, I am reported as a counter-voicer because the way in which this judgment is implemented is inadequate because it conflicts with the Austrian federal constitution. It is true that this law provides that the Federal Chancellery's right of information to the Data Protection Commission should be interrupted. The problem, however, is that Article 20 of the Austrian Federal Constitution says something completely different, namely that in the case of non-wisdom authorities – and that is the Data Protection Commission – there is at least the right to “read all the items of the management of the non-profit institutions”. – All objects are all objects and not just a part. To that end, the proposed solution, namely to limit the right of information within the meaning of the directive, is simply unconstitutional, and we are in principle not available for unconstitutional laws. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] There are two basic possibilities: The one of them, the Secretary of State, has tried to be honest in the Committee, namely a creative interpretation of the Constitution. He says: Yes, it is “all objects” in it, but the Austrian federal constitution cannot mean that it may include illegal objects against European law. – That’s creative, but it’s not enough for us. There would have been a second possibility that one could have said: Europe's law and constitutional law are collaborating here, so we must regulate it in accordance with constitutional law. In other words, we must adopt a constitutional provision with regard to the Data Protection Commission, which redefines the right to provide information, unlike with other non-wisdom authorities. You can't go this way. Why don't you go with him? – I can only think of it: If a two-thirds majority is on the agenda here in the house, then you need the opposition, and if you need the opposition for a two-thirds majority, then you will be negotiated; and obviously you didn't want to negotiate. I think it has a good reason why we did not want to negotiate on this matter: not because we would not be prepared to vote in favour of a two-thirds majority in principle, but for a completely different reason. If you talk about the Data Protection Commission, then you will inevitably end up with the main problem of the Data Protection Commission. The main problem of the Data Protection Commission is the lack of resources, and this abuse – and this is the main problem in the whole of Austrian data protection – can or will not be altered. I'm not alone with the analysis. That the opposition says there and there is maladministration, that is in the nature of the matter, that is our task. However, I would like to quote Mr Maier, who has a reputation as a data protection lawyer, although his voting behaviour has not always been able to keep up with the call. In the matter he sometimes says quite what is the matter, even if the difficulties arise – whether it is. I quote from an article: “New to the “Data Protection Commission” – that is, exactly what we are discussing today – “which had been forced by an ECJ judgment, Maier noted that the existing resources would hardly be achieved. It was necessary to ensure that sufficient personnel were available – in particular technicians were asked, as the Commission would also carry out on-the-spot checks in the future.” He put it to the point: Without solving the resource problem at the Data Protection Commission, Austrian data protection will not work. – And this would have been the subject of a two-thirds majority in the negotiations – I suppose, other parties too – so one has chosen this somewhat shaky and not very constitutional-compliant interpretation. In addition, this provision does not provide legal certainty, because the creative provision says nothing but that the right to provide information exists, but only so far as it does not contradict complete independence within the meaning of the directive. So it is said that the right of information is not affected, except as the directive says, one should be completely independent. That is, now we do not know much more than before, because when does the Data Protection Commission have to be completely independent now – or not? But I'm trying something now, Minister. I ask you a question, and you will then tell me whether such an information is still permitted in the future in the sense of the right of information, which continues to be restricted. In the course of the storage of data, the Data Protection Commission has the task of examining the providers subject to storage requirements. If we now want to know whether the Data Protection Commission fulfils this task and how many providers have been examined, and if the Federal Chancellor's Office asks how many providers have been examined, can this information still be given or does this contradict complete independence as required by the directive? Answer me this question! Because the law comes out of your house, it can't be a problem. - Thank you.

The professional review of cases where there is a suspicion of child welfare is ensured and standards of appropriate assistance are established. The current reform consists mainly of – in summary – the following measures: first of all, the introduction of risk clarification and assistance planning under the four-eye principle – which is very likely to be present and well explored; a re-forming of the notification obligation in the event of suspected child welfare risk; more precise definition of tasks and standards in the individual performance areas; and also a detailed regulation of confidentiality, information rights, documentation and data protection. The reform aims to improve the protection of children and young people from violence in the family and other risks; Impulses for uniform standards and further professionalization of professionals – that is immensely important, yes, we know that there are still difficulties, even in offices –; Strengthening the prevention of educational problems, an issue to which we should pay even more attention; prevention of parents in order to provide help to bring children to life; Specificisation of the objectives and tasks of child and youth assistance; and improvement of the protection of personal data. It's also important to co-finance the federal government, that's what you have to say. In 2013 and 2014, there are already targeted subsidies to increase the costs of the countries. I also hope that the countries will implement this law in the countries by April 2014 at the latest. I would also like to mention that the new law sets impulses for uniform standards regarding the authorisation of private institutions and social education institutions for the care of children, as well as for the assessment of the suitability of nursing parents and adoptive applicants. Finally, I can only ask or appeal to work together and hold together in spite of some different views with united forces for the protection and welfare of our children and young people. I would also like to express my thanks to the officials who have been trying for years to find a compromise with the countries. We should today in Parliament, if it is possible, decide upon this law.

With regard to the promotion of housing, I still hope for a joint decision on a commitment to the purpose of this legislative period. The housing funds provided for the creation of dwellings have a long tradition in Austria, which dates back to the time of the First Republic. In 1989, the housing aid grant law was put into force and then, in 2002, it has already been referred to today, changed into the purpose grant law, extending it to include measures of infrastructure and also the financing of measures to reduce greenhouse gases. At the same time, however, the return flows were no longer intended, which was a major mistake. Later – and this has been agreed under Minister of Finance Grasser – the target grant has been completely eliminated. At the same time, the loans have been sold since 2002, and this is far too cheap, one has to say, because the nominal value was only reached at 54 percent in these sales. This is, of course, a weakening of the financing power. There is less money available for housing. There were loans worth €10 billion that were sold there. Now on the request of the BZÖ, which again calls for a commitment of purpose, with the return to the legal situation of 2002. This is only a marginal improvement from our point of view, because it is a target binding light. I'll explain that too. They are removed, they are not included. The purpose is to restore measures to improve infrastructure and also to reduce greenhouse gas emissions. We Socialists are primarily concerned with the creation of a new, manageable housing, and that is crucial. [[Abg. Grosz: Yes, you can do that with the commitment of purpose!]] There are a number of other pots for improvement for thermal rehabilitation. There is, for example, the rehabilitation check, which is available, which unfortunately is not fully exploited. For example, when we look at the non-profit construction associations that have a very high rehabilitation rate, where 6 percent are reached per year, with over 90 percent of the apartments built before 1980 being thermally renovated, we see that this is also done with small, limited rents. That is why I wanted to mention this as an example. Housing has only a 13 percent share of greenhouse emissions, but transport of 27 percent, the industry of 29 percent. And from 1990 to 2002, the share of greenhouse emissions caused by housing and space heat fell significantly. That is why we reject this request because it does not seem appropriate for us to stimulate the development of housing. We are committed to a real commitment to the promotion of housing, to a self-sustaining system of financing, which creates security of living regardless of the economy, returning back from the loans and thus remaining tax money in the building pot and thus creating sedentary dwellings. This is an aspect, but it consists of several aspects. This can be read in our housing program. I call two more important demands from our point of view. These are transparent rental contracts with a limit of 25 percent of the surcharges and discounts – which would be around €6.50 in the Vienna area – and a abolition of the temporary contracts, whose number is of course too high, three-quarters of all new leases are limited. That is how I urge the coalition partner to implement this with us!

It made sense for us to have introduced housing: It is intended to reduce the price pressure that arises, the price increases that come to us every year. That's all the promotion of housing. It is simply a part of the total cost and slightly reduces the total cost of housing and thus reduces the costs for the owners or the tenants. That's the sense of housing promotion, and that's good. In recent times, for example, one has also invested in thermal renovation. This too is good, but does not go against the original purpose of keeping living as sedentary as possible. In the past, housing promotion was relatively much regulated by the federal government. At the beginning of the 1980s it was still such that the federal government laid down the rules and the countries simply executed them. In the 1980s, they were increasingly taken away from it, and the countries have cooked their own soups. In the meantime, housing promotion in each country is completely different. ParlaMint-AT_2013-03-21-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00194_d8e14682	I will resume the negotiations on points 17 to 21 of the agenda. Mrs Höllerer is speaking on behalf of the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-03-21-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00194_d8e14709	Madam President. Ladies and gentlemen, High house! Back to the Federal Children's and Youth Aid Act. After a long and very tough ring – which has already been addressed by previous speakers – a new Federal Children's and Youth Assistance Act is now being launched – a law that will improve the protection of children in Austria, a law that will bring about a change in the field of youth welfare, especially in the field of the four-eye principle. In the future, decisions will be made within the framework of a four-eye principle when particularly complex materials are available. The experts in youth welfare are, of course, confident to decide correctly for the benefit of the child, so that the four-eye principle is applied when there is contradictory information, difficult representations and, above all, very complex problems. If the child welfare seems to be threatened, two professionals should decide. That is to say, it is up to the experts to appreciate this properly, and that is of course their confidence. More simple decisions will, of course, continue to be made by a specialist, just as has been the case so far. I am convinced that this is a correct and important step in the right direction. There are, of course, other changes that have already been explained. It is about clarifying the particularly sensitive reporting and confidentiality obligations, which will bring greater legal certainty to families as well as to youth welfare partners. Regardless of the new federal uniform standards, it is left to the federal states to make stricter requirements in implementing legislation, i.e. to make other proposals in the field of the four-eye principle to put in place other measures. But once again a standard has been created which will, in any case, bring substantial improvements in the sense of youth welfare and will, above all, improve the protection of children. I would also like to say that youth welfare in the federal states is an important point of contact and, above all, sets out measures in the field of prevention and offers a wide range of services. This ranges from parental advice to support in addressing family problems to helping in difficult situations in life. But still the most important task of youth welfare is to provide the children with the protection they need, especially in acute situations of crisis. On the basis of the new Child and Youth Assistance Act, of course, it is also possible to improve the quality of child protection, especially because federal funding is provided. At this point it can be said: What's going on for a long time is going to be good at last! After years of preparatory work, and above all after the persistent endurance in the negotiations which Mr Mitterlehner has shown, Federal Minister Mitterlehner has now succeeded in adopting this Federal Children's and Youth Assistance Act. Perhaps one day a federal law on youth protection can also be established. I wish our Federal Minister Mitterlehner much success in the corresponding negotiations, especially much patience and endurance. Good luck!

ParlaMint-AT_2013-03-21-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00194_d8e16143	We're getting to the 24th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. Mr Principality is the first to speak. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2013-03-21-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00194_d8e16171	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! In 2006, the Federal Disability Equality Act adopted the reduction of structural barriers and a transitional period was agreed by the end of 2015. However, what was thought to be a sufficient transitional period was faced by many advisory bodies, because this year, in 2013, two years before the end of this transitional period, only about half of the 400 advisory services are accessible. There was therefore an urgent need for action. And thanks to Minister Mitterlehner, it is now possible with the innovation of the Family Advisory Act that from the beginning of the year to the year 2015, approximately € 1 million is available annually for the promotion of measures relating to disability justice and accessibility. Although this legal fixation and implementation does not originate from the Ministry of Family Affairs, the Ministry of Public Health has assumed its responsibility in terms of support for families and the elimination of discrimination. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Silhavy.]] Access to family advice is thus promoted for the first time in a targeted way. The government programme also clearly provides for the extension of accessibility to the family advisory services. Around 1.6 million people with disabilities, including many parents, grandparents and children, will thus be able to ensure that the advisory services are accessible freely from 2016 onwards. There are currently around 400 family advisory services in Austria. Thus, our country is practically comprehensively supplied with it. The number of discussions has increased from around 200 000 consultations in 1988 to around 435,000 in 2010. A consultation meeting takes an average of 45 minutes. Family services help families in emergencies and support families in a professional way in dealing with the crisis and, for example, in resolving internal family conflicts. I would therefore like to take advantage of this opportunity to thank all the professionals who provide valuable service to people in the family advisory services. With their daily work, professional advice, and simply by listening and listening to problems and through patience, they help people in distress. Thank you for that! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP.]] Family advice, which is offered free of charge, needs-based and close to people, namely in a more immediate housing environment, costs money. The total cost of financing the advisory bodies is almost € 50 million per year. In 2013, around €12.6 million will be paid out of the Family Compensation Fund, which is €1 million more than in the last budget estimate. And exactly this € 1 million is used to enable Austrian-wide implementation of accessibility. There is no additional burden on the budget, but it is ensured and ensured that all the advisory bodies can be reached by the year 2016 without any other assistance. If this were not possible, the consequences would be fatal, especially for assistance and advice seekers in rural areas, because the family advisory services there It would have to be closed by the end of 2015. I am therefore particularly pleased that this proposal for a law was unanimously approved by the Family Committee. In doing so, we show all parents of disabled children and disabled parents how important we are to their concerns and accessible access to the family services.

The question was also raised in the room: Can one apply several times? – We assume that, in fact, with an application, as the problem is already apparent, it can also be found out and can be done. A question that has not yet been raised in the Committee was also: Is this a purpose assignment, or can it also be used for something else? – It is no purpose. We assume, however, that we will actually find out exactly with this measure and its clarification. So the question of whether one can use it for personal support will not be asked in practice with relatively high probability. The final effect is therefore a pragmatic approach in order to be able to give real support to the relevant advisory bodies. As to whether all were informed and are: In our area, yes. Also where women's advice is offered by the family counsellors, yes. The other implementation, where, for example, pure women's houses offer consulting services and support, we do not fall within our competence area. I cannot therefore answer these questions. However, I assume that there has also been a provision for the reconstruction measures and that they have ensured appropriate support. In this sense, thank you very much for your approval.

[[18:22:51]] 28. Point Re-election of committees
ParlaMint-AT_2013-03-21-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00194_d8e18723	We're coming to 28. Agenda item. As a result of the change in the force ratio of the clubs, a new composition of the committees elected in accordance with section 32 of the Rules of Procedure is achieved with 26 members. After consultation with the parliamentary clubs, the committees will be re-elected. It is proposed that the Committee size of the committees affected by the change should be fixed with 26 members and replacement members. The division of committee members and substitute members among the clubs is now calculated according to the d’Hondt’s system as follows: Each 9 members and replacement members shall be the members of the SPÖ, 8 members and replacement members of the ÖVP, 5 members and replacement members of the FPÖ, 3 members and replacement members of the Greens each, 1 member each, replacement member to the BZÖ. A re-election of the main committee does not take place, as no change has yet been made as regards the electoral list within the meaning of § 30 GOG. We shall come to the vote. I shall vote on the setting of the Committee size with 26 members and replacement members in turn, and I shall seek the opinion of the Committee. Ladies and gentlemen, who are here for a sign of approval. – This is unanimously accepted. The names of the members of the Committee, or alternate members, who have been announced by the President's clubs, and thus as elected Members, are to be found in the list of committees. [[The members and substitute members are available on the Internet under the heading ‘Parliament Activ> Committees>National Council> Committees and Subcommittees’.]] Part of the newly elected committees will be agreed by the members of the Presidential Conference on 3 March. April 2013, at 10.30 p.m. The agenda is exhausted. [[18:24:35]] Reading of a part of the Official Protocol

I shall now read the corresponding part of the Official Protocol: ‘TO point 28: Re-election of committees As a result of the changes in the force ratio of the clubs, a new composition of the committees with 26 members results. The Committee size of the committees concerned by the amendment shall be fixed unanimously with 26 members and replacement members. The SPÖs now account for 9, for the ÖVPs each 8, for the FPÖs each 5, for the Greens per 3 members and replacement members as well as for the BZÖs each 1 member and replacement member. There is a desire according to § 51 para. 6 GOG by 20 Members to read the Official Protocol on Agenda item 28.’; Are there any objections to the version or contents of the Official Protocol? - That's not the case.
